Status: ACTIVE / CANONICAL / SUPERSEDES CONFLICTING ACTIVE OCR INSTRUCTIONS BELOW.
The dated source records and historical test reports below remain unchanged as
provenance. Do not treat historical settings/results as current policy.

A. CANONICAL SOURCE AND EXACT CURRENT BASE
- Repository: https://github.com/pbrau014/braultDwBotVis.git
- Sole active branch: main.
- Verified main HEAD immediately before this plan update:
  b5c2a0d3c2b1adcd51ef644886da18cb1ca5818c.
- Relevant current commits:
  * 56f26a190ee5fcb5df2da10650d477a37423809a — explicitly require OCR.Space
    activation and permit the approved provider network path.
  * fceaf6e3bd28f3746c737e7306747f490088bbdf — corrected the PDF-first
    contract test to require the hidden-but-searchable PDF text layer and
    assert that the returned PDF bytes are retained.
  * 7d451dca07a832c44b0d6ee6c6622bdef59a46a2 — synchronized the README's
    active OCR architecture and activation instructions.
  * b5c2a0d3c2b1adcd51ef644886da18cb1ca5818c — synchronized the OCR.Space
    implementation guide's explicit-enable and provider-network policy.
- The PDF-first implementation is present in canonical main. These source and
  documentation commits are implementation evidence, not live-provider,
  exact-corpus, Windows-acceptance, or release acceptance.
- The current GitHub code-search connector returned no indexed results for
  targeted OCRSPACE_ENABLED / network / PDF-first searches despite known files
  being present. Relevant source, test, workflow, environment examples, README,
  implementation guide and living plan were fetched directly. Do not claim an
  exhaustive scan of every tracked blob from that search response.

B. BINDING OCR.SPACE PRIORITY AND EXPLICIT ACTIVATION
- OCR.Space is the highest-priority and first text-extraction stage for every
  eligible original image within the explicitly authorized DWBOT screenshot
  inventories. Do not describe it as optional, secondary, last-resort,
  diagnostic-only, or disabled in the active implementation.
- Every eligible original image must enter the PDF-first path with
  OCRSPACE_ENABLED=1, OCRSPACE_NETWORK_ALLOWLIST=1 and a valid local
  OCRSPACE_API_KEY. Production startup and the Windows OCR acceptance wrapper
  require explicit enablement and the key. A missing key or disabled provider
  blocks that image; it does not authorize local OCR of the original image.
- OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0 is permitted only for isolated offline/mock tests that do
  not transmit an original image, or for downstream local-reader invocations
  after the successful PDF-first stage has already produced the raster. In the
  latter case it prevents a duplicate provider request; the input is already
  derived from the OCR.Space PDF. It is never a local-original-image fallback
  for an eligible source screenshot.
- Keep the Free API only. Keep the key local or in protected Actions secrets;
  never place it in chat, source, logs, reports, artifacts, or packages. Paid
  tiers/engines/usage remain unauthorized. Reserve quota before requests,
  fail closed on quota-ledger errors, and do not automatically retry.

C. REQUIRED SOURCE IMAGE → SEARCHABLE PDF → LOCAL READER SEQUENCE
1. Submit each eligible original World of Warships screenshot to OCR.Space
   Engine 2 first. Engine 2 is the active PDF-producing engine because the
   provider's current comparison matrix marks searchable-PDF creation
   supported for Engine 2 and unsupported for Engine 3.
2. Make one OCR.Space request and obtain one searchable PDF per source image per processing/acceptance pass. Do not make separate crop/field requests. All local readers in that pass reuse the PDF-derived raster. Separate engine-comparison passes may create distinct PDF artifacts; record each request and do not imply one provider request covered multiple independently launched passes.
   The searchable text layer is hidden from the rendered page to avoid
   duplicating/obscuring the page image while remaining searchable.
3. Download the returned PDF immediately from an approved HTTPS OCR.Space host.
   Reject redirects, unapproved hosts, invalid/non-PDF payloads, oversized
   responses and timeouts. Preserve source, normalized-upload, PDF and raster
   SHA-256 values, byte sizes, dimensions, engine, provider status and timings.
4. Preserve the PDF and its PDF-derived raster as per-image acceptance artifacts.
   The Windows acceptance wrapper sets OCRSPACE_ARTIFACT_DIR to a per-run
   directory; the 64-image inventory runner writes each PDF/raster into its
   output directory. Every acceptance run must retain and hash the artifacts.
   In the normal bot path, durable artifact writing is conditional on
   OCRSPACE_ARTIFACT_DIR being configured; set it for any run requiring
   persistent evidence. Do not claim durable PDF preservation if no file and
   checksum were produced. No local API key or live provider PDF is verified
   in this chat.
5. Render the downloaded PDF page locally to a bounded PNG raster. Tesseract.js,
   native Tesseract, optional PaddleOCR, OpenCV geometry and every downstream
   local OCR/text-extraction reader operate only on this PDF-derived raster.
   They must never return to the original source image after the PDF-first
   stage begins. This is the supported reader input contract; the OCR engines
   consume the PDF page rendering because they are image readers.
6. Keep OCR.Space response text and the PDF's searchable text layer separate
   from local OCR candidates. They are provenance/diagnostic evidence and may
   not independently accept, merge, rewrite, normalize, fuzzy-correct or
   override a mandatory field. Existing parsers, geometry, independent-read
   consensus and exact validation remain authoritative.
7. If OCR.Space processing, PDF creation/download, PDF validation, artifact
   preservation required by the run, or rasterization fails, fail that image
   closed. Never fall back to OCR of the original screenshot.

D. NETWORK POLICY — PROVIDER ACCESS ENABLED
- OCRSPACE_NETWORK_ALLOWLIST=1 explicitly permits HTTPS access to
  api.ocr.space for the API request and the approved OCR.Space PDF host
  api.ocr.space / ocr.space for the temporary PDF download.
- The network guard must never block these approved OCR.Space requests. No
  blanket offline/network-blocking setting may be applied to the provider path.
- OCR_NON_OCR_NETWORK_RESTRICTED=1, when set, restricts and logs only unrelated
  outbound destinations. It does not block OCR.Space. Keep this least-privilege
  restriction rather than opening arbitrary outbound access. "Network blocking
  disabled" in the active OCR rule means OCR.Space's required network path is
  enabled, not that all unrelated network destinations become unrestricted.
- Acceptance diagnostics must distinguish provider requests from unrelated
  denied egress and must record both accurately. A zero blocked-attempt count
  does not prove that a provider request occurred.

E. PRIVACY, SCOPE, AND NINE-FIELD ACCEPTANCE
- The user-authorized full-image scope remains limited to the named 4-image,
  16-image and 64-image World of Warships inventories, including visible
  player names in those inventories. Do not transmit unrelated images, private
  account data, Discord IDs, credentials or unrelated UI. No production
  screenshot transmission, live Discord action, paid usage or deployment is
  authorized by this instruction. Production remains NO-GO.
- Normalize images above the Free API 1 MiB limit locally without modifying
  the original; preserve original/upload hashes and dimensions.
- The exact mandatory order remains result, player, map, mode, battleStart,
  credits, freeXp, damage, xp. XP is ninth and mandatory. Zero wrongly
  accepted mandatory values is required; Gate A additionally requires zero
  unresolved mandatory fields. Do not tune against Gate B or alter ground truth.
- Preserve provider terms/privacy/retention review, HTTPS/redirect restrictions,
  response bounds, timeout, quota, no-retry, key secrecy, artifact provenance,
  and fail-closed behavior.

F. CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION / VERIFICATION STATUS
- Direct source inspection confirmed src/ocrspace-pdf-first.js requests Engine 2
  on the original image, requests a searchable PDF, validates and downloads the
  PDF, rasterizes it, and returns the PDF/raster bytes and hashes.
- src/bot.js calls the PDF-first stage before OpenCV/Tesseract and replaces its
  downstream image buffer with the PDF-derived raster. It writes PDF/raster
  artifacts when OCRSPACE_ARTIFACT_DIR is configured.

CURRENT OCR ARCHITECTURE OVERRIDE — OCR.SPACE SOURCE-IMAGE → SEARCHABLE PDF FIRST
2026-11-05 / controlling user instruction
================================================================================

The user's latest instruction in this chat supersedes older active OCR pipeline descriptions. Historical reports and prior designs remain unchanged as provenance.

REQUIRED PIPELINE
1. Receive the original World of Warships battle-result screenshot.
2. OCR.Space Engine 2 processes that source image FIRST and creates a searchable
   PDF with a searchable text layer hidden from the rendered page so local OCR readers do not see duplicate overlay text. The provider's current engine comparison marks
   searchable-PDF creation supported for Engine 2 and unsupported for Engine 3.
   Engine 2 is therefore the PDF-producing first pass.
3. Immediately download the temporary PDF. Validate HTTPS host, redirect
   behavior, PDF signature, response size and timeout. Record source SHA-256,
   normalized-upload SHA-256, PDF SHA-256, sizes, dimensions, engine and timing.
4. Render the PDF page locally to a bounded PNG raster. Tesseract.js, native
   Tesseract, PaddleOCR (when enabled), OpenCV geometry and every downstream
   local image reader must operate on this PDF-derived raster, never on the
   original screenshot after successful PDF creation.
5. Keep OCR.Space provider text/PDF text-layer evidence separate. Local readers
   independently recognize the PDF raster. Do not inject remote text into local
   candidates or accept remote-only values.
6. Apply existing parsers, field geometry, independent-read consensus and exact
   validators. Nine mandatory fields remain: result, player, map, mode,
   battleStart, credits, freeXp, damage, xp. XP is ninth and mandatory. Any
   wrong accepted mandatory value fails; missing, ambiguous, conflicting or
   invalid values remain unresolved. Gate A/B require zero wrong accepts and
   zero unresolved required fields.

FAIL-CLOSED AND PRIVACY RULES
- If OCR.Space processing, searchable-PDF creation/download or rasterization
  fails, fail that image. Never fall back to OCR on the original image.
- Production requires OCRSPACE_API_KEY. The bot refuses normal startup without
  it. OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0 is an explicit local-only development/test override.
- Use the Free API only. Current docs list a 1 MiB per-file limit, 500 requests
  per day, 25,000 per month and three PDF pages. Free searchable PDFs have a
  watermark. PDF URLs last one hour; uploads are deleted after processing,
  PDFs after 60 minutes, and API IP logs after one month. Recheck current terms.
- Images over 1 MiB are locally re-encoded to bounded JPEG. Preserve original
  hash and upload hash/size/dimensions/quality. Never modify the source image.
- The user explicitly authorized OCR.Space processing of the 4-, 16- and
  64-image World of Warships screenshot inventories in this chat, including
  visible player names. Authorization is limited to those inventories. Never
  send credentials, Discord IDs, private account data, unrelated UI or images.
  Never expose the API key in logs, reports, artifacts or source.
- Restrict acceptance egress to HTTPS OCR.Space API/PDF hosts. Block and log all
  other destinations. Never auto-retry rate-limited requests. Quota reservation
  remains fail-closed.

PRIME DIRECTIVE — GOVERNING RULE FOR ALL PLAN IMPLEMENTATION
================================================================================

The user authorizes appropriate implementation work within the clearly approved
DWBOT plan scope, including reviews, corrections, edits, testing and verification.
Use valid credentials and authenticated workflows with least privilege. This is
not permission to bypass authentication, authorization, role checks, access
controls, platform safeguards, rate limits, privacy/security controls, legal
requirements, or mandatory acceptance/release gates.

Never spoof, simulate, disable, evade, or bypass a required control to make
progress appear complete. If a required credential, role, approval, environment
or permission is missing, record that specific dependency as BLOCKED and
continue every independent authorized task. The blocked action resumes when
its required condition is available; unrelated work continues.

A requirement is fulfilled only when its implementation, review and verification
evidence is complete against the exact source and runtime where applicable.
Authorization, a code change, a mock, a queued/running job, or an unverified
report is not proof of acceptance. Preserve source/evidence provenance and
distinguish attempted, completed, tested, verified and accepted. This Prime
Directive governs every task, recommendation, implementation decision, handoff
and release decision in this plan. Where lower-level text conflicts, preserve
required controls and document the conflict rather than bypassing them.

RECENT MAIN IMPLEMENTATION CHANGES
- 7fe2d9721eef6d08d0085e00f2d524f7e5c8d8f5: require verified identity-boundary
  geometry for OCR.Space field crops. Both the OpenCV prepass and last-resort
  local-crop filter mark a crop eligible only after checking it against the
  identity ROI. Unverified crop plans are rejected before crop construction,
  quota reservation or provider calls.
- 5d6478bfe6a2125ff182ac72098727c449541e25: corrected the two scaled/unscaled
  identity-overlap regression fixtures.
- a78de416480acd4c09009dc128563ec386582c07 and
  0dd3fd4314c433b58e546ca8a292aae5e7031408: fixed the Windows OCR acceptance
  wrapper's native-stderr handling. Windows PowerShell 5.1 with
  ErrorActionPreference=Stop could turn the OCR supervisor's normal stderr
  close message into a terminating NativeCommandError after the child exited
  successfully. The wrapper now redirects stdout and stderr to separate
  temporary files, retains both in the command transcript, streams stdout,
  and cleans both temporary files. A static regression test checks the contract.
  This fix is committed but is not yet verified by a completed Windows run.
- 3fdacf32401e5bbbe0720db38e0a2b2959938ee1 and
  3f0eee8112d304a63ec8d3f234ff0acbc12766f0: tighten reward/damage conflict
  handling. Numeric rewards require two agreeing isolated reads; independently
  corroborated prefix/suffix conflicts trigger geometry adjudication or
  abstention; broad-only reward guesses are no longer retained when localized
  geometry is unavailable. A geometry-bounded damage crop conflict blocks a
  conflicting broad full-screen consensus unless a specific geometry quorum
  repair rule proves the value.
- d7a1e99edb500b6423689789aad61b2e5714d391: deduplicate broad OCR conflict
  evidence by PSM/source and make geometry-anchor support checks use the same
  consolidated conflict-candidate set. These changes tighten conflict provenance;
  they are not screenshot-accuracy acceptance evidence.

A. CURRENT MAIN AND OCR.SPACE DEFAULT-ON IMPLEMENTATION
- Sole canonical repository: pbrau014/braultDwBotVis, branch main. Main HEAD
  before this plan refresh: 14ca84b3dcf21f9e0f24efe6e6d60da38c98de65.
- Code commit 3ad588fc0b4ed3923ca76703b78edd87d73bea74 implements default-on
  activation when a local Free API key is configured. An unset/blank
  OCRSPACE_ENABLED value or 1 enables; explicit 0 disables; invalid non-empty
  values fail closed; a key is mandatory. The helper getOcrSpaceActivation in
  src/ocrspace-field-evidence.js is used by src/bot.js and
  src/ocr-report-metadata.js. Regression tests cover key-only default
  activation, explicit opt-out, missing key and invalid setting.
- Documentation commit 57646d07306e22f439229e96dcdd77cc729d0c81 updates the
  desktop and Discord test environment examples, README, OCR.Space guide,
  handoff and authority rules. Roadmap commit
  14ca84b3dcf21f9e0f24efe6e6d60da38c98de65 carries the new rules into plan1.
- This changes the remote activation default only. It does not remove the local
  key requirement or any safe-crop, identity-exclusion, HTTPS, redirect, size,
  timeout, quota, no-retry or fail-closed safeguard. The Windows local-primary
  acceptance script intentionally sets OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0; retain that explicit
  local-only override.
- No local OCR.Space key is configured in this session. No live API request or
  screenshot transmission has occurred. Mock tests do not prove live provider
  accuracy or live terms/privacy compliance.

1. REMOTE OCR.SPACE DEFAULT — USER-AUTHORIZED CHANGE
- OCR.Space remote use is DEFAULT-ON whenever a local Free API key is configured.
- An unset or blank OCRSPACE_ENABLED value means the default-on behavior.
  OCRSPACE_ENABLED=1 explicitly enables; OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0 is an explicit
  local-only opt-out; any other non-empty value fails closed.
- The local OCRSPACE_API_KEY remains mandatory. No key means no provider request.
  The key must remain local, untracked, unlogged and absent from packages.
- That earlier statement described a historical local-only baseline. The current
  Windows OCR acceptance wrapper sets OCRSPACE_ENABLED=1, requires the API key,
  and allows only OCR.Space HTTPS API/PDF hosts. Local-only tests must explicitly
  set OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0.
- PDF-first processing still enforces size/time/quota limits, fixed HTTPS
  destination, redirect rejection, no automatic retries and fail-closed behavior.
  The current user explicitly authorizes full screenshots, including visible
  player names, for the 4-, 16- and 64-image World of Warships inventories only.
  Credentials, Discord identifiers, private account data and unrelated UI or
  screenshots remain prohibited.
- Tesseract.js/native Tesseract remain local readers after PDF rasterization.
  OCR.Space output remains separate diagnostic evidence. The independent
  src/ocrspace-reconciliation.js cross-check reports agreement, disagreement,
  remote-only, remote-conflict and missing-candidate states; it never mutates
  the local record or promotes remote-only candidates into acceptance.

2. ENGINE 2 SEARCHABLE-PDF FIRST-PASS RULE — CURRENT OVERRIDE
- The provider comparison matrix marks searchable-PDF creation supported for
  Engine 2 and unsupported for Engine 3. Use Engine 2 directly for the first
  source-image request; do not use Engine 3 first or as a PDF fallback.
- Request one searchable PDF per original screenshot with a hidden but
  searchable text layer. Validate and download it, then render the page locally.
  All local OCR readers process the PDF-derived raster.
- This replaces the earlier Engine 3-first / Engine 2 fallback description.

3. ENTERPRISEOCRPIPELINE.PY — ROOT-FILE REVIEW
- The root file is a standalone Python/FastAPI/SQLAlchemy document-OCR service
  prototype, not the existing Node.js Discord bot's OCR implementation.
  It uses PaddleOCR as primary recognition and optional native Tesseract CLI
  comparison, plus image normalization/deskew, layout reconstruction, table
  extraction, entity parsing, page/document persistence, a review endpoint,
  metrics and webhook-outbox code.
- The identical duplicate at services/cv_ocr/EnterpriseOCRPipeline.py has the
  same Git blob SHA cadf83d1627f69120f2442e5ccdb27a97108598c (61,235 bytes).
  The tracked tree has no tasks.py/worker module for the tasks import/queue call,
  no dedicated Enterprise service dependency manifest, and no tests for this
  service. The upload route therefore cannot be treated as a complete runnable
  pipeline from the current tracked files.
- The wows profile does not implement DWBOT's strict nine-field battle-record
  parsing/acceptance. It is not wired into src/bot.js and would replace or
  bypass the existing Node/Tesseract.js architecture if adopted as-is.
- Useful reusable ideas: bounded Paddle model pooling, local preprocessing and
  deskew, text-block geometry/reading order, table extraction, optional local
  Tesseract comparison, metrics and page-level evidence persistence. These may
  inform isolated benchmarks or carefully ported modules after OCR.Space gates.
- Do not integrate or deploy this service as-is. Before any future reuse, resolve
  its missing worker/dependency packaging, add tests, enforce authentication on
  document retrieval/upload, verify review candidate identifiers, and establish
  an explicit nine-field DWBOT adapter that preserves local acceptance authority.
  The root and duplicate files remain unchanged in this review.

C. OCR.SPACE REQUEST PROFILE — CURRENT PDF-FIRST REQUIREMENTS

- Original source image is the first OCR input to OCR.Space Engine 2.
- Required request: OCREngine=2, detectOrientation=true, scale=true,
  isTable=false, isCreateSearchablePdf=true, isSearchablePdfHideTextLayer=true.
  The hidden text layer remains searchable but is not painted into the raster
  page, avoiding duplicate visible text during local OCR.
- The Free API accepts at most 1 MiB per image. Larger source images are
  re-encoded locally with a bounded size/quality profile. Preserve original
  source SHA-256 and normalized upload SHA-256, dimensions, quality and bytes.
- Download SearchablePDFURL immediately. Allow only HTTPS api.ocr.space or
  ocr.space; reject redirects, invalid URLs, non-PDF signatures, oversized
  responses and timeouts. Record PDF and rendered raster hashes/bytes/dimensions.
- Render the PDF page locally to PNG. Tesseract.js, native Tesseract, optional
  PaddleOCR and OpenCV geometry operate on this PDF-derived raster only.
- OCR.Space parsed text and searchable text layer remain separate provider
  evidence. They do not enter local OCR candidate arrays or acceptance. The
  active bot makes one source-image request per screenshot and no crop request.
- Fail closed if API, PDF creation/download or rasterization fails. Production
  requires OCRSPACE_API_KEY. OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0 is explicit local-only mode.
  Free tier only; no automatic retries, paid endpoint or quota bypass.
- The earlier Engine 3-first / Engine 2 PDF-fallback and crop-first profiles are
  historical and superseded.

D. IMPLEMENTATION MAP AND CURRENT VERIFICATION STATE

Current active implementation:
- src/ocrspace-pdf-first.js: Engine 2 source-image request, searchable PDF
  creation, safe PDF download, PDF signature/size checks and raster handoff.
- src/image-processor.js: <=1 MiB upload normalization and bounded PDF-page render.
- src/bot.js: PDF-first request before local readers; local readers process only
  the PDF-derived raster; fail-closed on configured errors; no crop refinement.
- src/ocrspace-client.js: defaults to Engine 2 and hidden searchable text layer.
  Engine 3 remains only for legacy non-PDF diagnostics.
- src/ocrspace-field-evidence.js and src/ocrspace-prepass.js: legacy crop-only
  helpers/tests, not called by the active bot.
- src/ocrspace-reconciliation.js: provider text remains separate diagnostic
  evidence and cannot overwrite local candidates or accepted fields.
- src/ocrspace-quota-ledger.js: fail-closed per-day/per-month reservation.
- src/ocr-report-metadata.js: reports Engine 2 PDF-first sequence and limits.
- scripts/block-network.js: HTTPS allowlist for api.ocr.space and ocr.space only.
- scripts/run-windows-ocr-acceptance.ps1: OCR.Space enabled for 4/16 corpora,
  requires the API key, and conditionally invokes the 64-image runner.
- scripts/run-ocrspace-64-inventory.js: verifies exact ZIP and all members
  before one PDF per image; local Tesseract.js/native readers consume the raster.
- tests/ocrspace-pdf-first.test.js and tests/ocr-report-metadata.test.js verify
  the new contract. Live provider/Windows/inventory acceptance is unverified.

Historical CI and test reports below remain bound to their recorded source
fingerprints. They are not current PDF-first acceptance evidence.

0.5 OCR.SPACE — PRIMARY DEVELOPMENT FOCUS (CURRENT BINDING USER RULE)

- OCR.Space Engine 2 processes the original World of Warships screenshot first
  and creates a searchable PDF with a hidden-but-searchable text layer.
- Download/validate the PDF, render its page locally to a bounded raster, then
  run Tesseract.js/native Tesseract/PaddleOCR/OpenCV on that PDF-derived raster.
  No local reader processes the original screenshot after successful PDF creation.
- One OCR.Space request/PDF per screenshot. No crop-based remote follow-up.
  Remote OCR.Space text remains separate diagnostic evidence; local parsers,
  geometry, consensus and exact validators determine accepted values.
- Failure of the API, PDF creation/download or rasterization fails closed.
  Production requires OCRSPACE_API_KEY; OCRSPACE_ENABLED=0 is local-only.

  names, for the 4-, 16- and 64-image World of Warships inventories only.
  Unrelated images, credentials, Discord IDs and private account data remain
  excluded. Free API only; <=1 MiB normalized uploads; quota and HTTPS allowlist.

  The 64-image runner verifies imgsHalf1.zip (57,665,767 bytes,

## Phase 3 RC3 targeted implementation and current go/no-go — 2026-10-25

**Current status: targeted implementation complete in the separate RC3 work copy; local focused tests PASS; full Windows OCR acceptance NOT RUN on this source; production NO-GO.** Original RC2 package remains unchanged. The matching implementation report is `PHASE-3-IMPLEMENTATION-REPORT-RC3.md`; focused test and syntax logs are `PHASE-3-IMPLEMENTATION-FOCUSED-TESTS.log` and `PHASE-3-IMPLEMENTATION-SYNTAX.log`. The 50-file RC3 source fingerprint is `1dd915e1a457a51eca5c2ca1c039697647643a274d6107cacb831e1ca81183ab`.

### What changed in the RC3 work copy

1. **Separated lifecycle from OCR exactness.** The acceptance launcher runs a dedicated Asashio known-good fixture with `scripts/integrated-ocr-stall-recovery.js --fixture=asashio-defeat.jpg --lifecycle-only`. It requires a real recognition before the injected second-call stall, timeout logging, confirmed worker termination, old-child close, fresh recovery-child close, a valid output payload and zero network attempts. The reported screenshot remains a separate end-to-end exact-field gate; a successful process recovery must not hide unresolved damage.
2. **Hardened the real negative control.** The launcher first requires a complete exact-match Asashio baseline in the same runtime. The negative run changes only expected damage from `171577` to `171578`; actual OCR values must remain byte-for-byte identical, with damage the only failed comparison and a nonzero exit. This fixes the previous weakness where an already-missing damage value could make the negative control look successful.
3. **Added cross-pass reward geometry for Credits, XP and Free XP.** New `src/reward-geometry.js` anchors field crops from independent OCR word boxes, joins tightly adjacent tokens as geometry-only hints (e.g. split XP/Free XP), and creates three width variants. The caller reads each variant under PSM 8 and 13 and only accepts a unique group with at least four matching reads spanning two widths and both PSMs. If a field disagrees with its row anchor and no unique crop quorum exists, that field remains missing and broad tuple fallback is prevented from restoring a disputed value. This is evidence-based re-reading, not expected-value injection.
4. **Added cautious leading map-artifact cleanup.** `src/battle-record.js` removes leading result-banner fragments `Victor`/`Victory`/`Defeat` and leading 2–5 character all-capital OCR debris before the remaining map name. This targets the observed `Victor Mountain Range` and `ATRAT Crash Zone Alpha` patterns without setting a map to a known expected value.
5. **Added regression coverage and acceptance linkage.** New `tests/reward-geometry.test.js` exercises fragmented tokens from the Hoffmann, Libertad and Schlieffen patterns, crop boundaries, and field digit bounds. A new battle-record test covers the two map debris patterns. The acceptance launcher includes both tests in its unit/contract stage. The revised OCR acceptance launcher now comprises **12 stages** (the former 10 plus a separately verified known-good negative-control baseline and an isolated lifecycle-only recovery stage).
6. **Damage selection remains fail-closed.** Existing independent full-screen damage consensus tests include the `87373` versus `873131` conflict. No expected damage or ground-truth field was hardcoded and exact-match checks were not weakened.

### Verification on the available environment

- Linux x64 Node `v22.16.0`, npm `10.9.2` is available here; the package requires Node.js `>=24.17.0`. `dotenv`, `discord.js`, and `tesseract.js` are missing from the incomplete local `node_modules`. [Current runtime authorization: Node.js v22.16.0 is acceptable; Node.js 24+ is not a prerequisite. Preserve this historical result as recorded.]
- Focused targeted regression command: **60 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, exit 0. It covered battle-record, reward geometry, damage consensus, localized numeric parsing, OCR process supervision/watchdog, serial queue, Windows path/cancellation helpers, and acceptance negative-control tests.
- `node --check` across `.js` files under `src/`, `scripts/` and `tests/`: exit 0.
- **Not performed / not a pass:** full `npm test`, real `npm run test:tesseractjs`, actual OCR on the changed code, or Windows Node 24 validation. Since dependencies are missing and the available runtime is unsupported, do not count the focused 60 as the full suite. No fresh OCR accuracy claims are made for these modifications. [Current runtime authorization: Node.js v22.16.0 is acceptable; Node.js 24+ is not a prerequisite. Preserve this historical result as recorded.]

## Exact Windows RC2 generated-report analysis — 2026-10-25 (historical input to RC3 implementation)

**Historical decision for RC2: Gate A PASS; Gate B FAIL.** The exact generated Windows reports were the evidence used to scope the later RC3 work. This section records the RC2 failure diagnosis and is not an RC3 validation result. The detailed field-level review is `PHASE-3-RC2-EXACT-WINDOWS-REPORT-ANALYSIS.md`.

### Runtime and acceptance-stage outcome

- Evidence bundle `RC2-Windows-failing-reports.zip` SHA-256: `64c7f68aa5896e120a6f8e8f255ac4759b187380998c88ad51101157c3d2e42b`. The acceptance report sidecar matches the JSON SHA-256 `92e3170a9aa8828cc2d4d3978f1293503f700905e500726c619d5a03e0a3d6f3`.
- Exact run `2026-10-25T11-24-45-302Z-18268`: Windows x64 / Node `v24.22.1` / npm `11.19.1`, Tesseract.js `6.0.1`, core `6.1.2`; source-build fingerprint `29e53696370010351177aa9b46e9c2d5b26b8bed6eef5fe9d5a85b19df258021` over 48 files. Local assets passed. `nativeTesseractAvailable: false`.
- The acceptance command passed **3/10 stages**: unit/contracts, local-asset check, and the deliberate-wrong-expected stage. The remaining seven failed. Network blocking was enabled and no blocked attempts were detected.
- The negative-control stage is green by the report, but its diagnostic strength is limited: actual damage was already `null` because the baseline extraction was ambiguous. Harden the negative control using an otherwise exact passing fixture and a single incorrect expected value.

### Exact diagnosis of the integrated recovery failure

- The first supervised child completed genuine recognition on pass 1, then the test injected a stall during pass 2. The 15-second `recognize` deadline fired (`OCR_OPERATION_TIMEOUT`), worker termination was confirmed, the first child close was confirmed, and a fresh recovery child returned `ok: true` with its close confirmed.
- The integrated test then failed at the assertion that the real recovery screenshot must pass exact-field comparison (`scripts/integrated-ocr-stall-recovery.js:147`). It failed because damage remains unresolved: focused PSM 8 read `873131` at confidence 0 from a non-isolated/broad crop, while full-screen PSM 3 read `87373` at confidence 79. The selection remained null, preserving fail-closed behavior. Thus cleanup/restart is evidenced; the full integrated stage still correctly fails on recovered-result accuracy/completeness. Do not classify this as a recovery process hang.

### Per-fixture failures and how to act on them

- **Reported screenshot:** 8/9 fields match; only damage fails. Its width-sweep diagnostics return `873` at crop widths `0.096`/`0.098` and `87373` at `0.100` under PSM 8 and 13, both at confidence 32. This is only two PSM modes at one width and does not meet the existing multi-width isolated-crop quorum; the fail-closed null is explicable. Also investigate why PSM 11 raw full-screen text contains `87373` but is absent from `fullScreenDamageCandidates`. Do not hardcode the expected number or weaken acceptance.
- **Hoffmann:** result/player/date/Credits/damage match; map is unresolved due `Mountain Range` vs `Victor Mountain Range`; XP is `14` vs `14390`; Free XP is `390` vs `1645`. The localized reward reads are truncated/low-confidence. Inspect map candidate normalization and reward geometry/parser before changing selection.
- **Asashio:** exact 9/9 in both orders. Damage selection succeeds through an isolated focused-crop quorum at multiple widths (`0.096` and `0.098`) and PSM 8/13. Use it as a known-good fixture to isolate Phase 3's post-timeout lifecycle check.
- **Libertad:** player confuses `O`/`0`; Credits is missing (`511113` expected; localized crop reads `91111`/`51111`); XP `113` vs `4509`; Free XP `45095` vs `608`; damage is ambiguous (`227678`, `221010`, and full-screen `2276787`). Investigate per-field geometry, reward columns, appended adjacent UI digits, and narrowly supported player glyph normalization.
- **Schlieffen:** result/player/mode/date/Credits/damage match; map is unresolved (`Crash Zone Alpha` vs `ATRAT Crash Zone Alpha`); XP `2` vs `2653`; Free XP `653` vs `325`. Broad OCR contains `2653`-/`325`-like material but local reward reads truncate/contaminate values; XP geometry sweep was not attempted. Test reward-column and map candidate logic independently.
- All three five-image runs completed five jobs but only **1/5 exact** and **1/5 complete required fields**, with the same result each time. Both order tests show Asashio passes and Hoffmann fails identically. This is deterministic fixture/field-specific accuracy evidence, not a general hang or evidence of queue-order contamination.

### Revised recommendation before broader Phase 3 work

1. Preserve the uploaded exact JSON reports and their hashes; do not rerun Gate A unnecessarily. Keep Gate B FAIL/OPEN and production NO-GO.
2. In a separate work copy, separate Phase 3 lifecycle assertions from Phase 4 exact-field accuracy: prove injected stall, timeout, confirmed worker termination, old-child close, fresh-child start-after-close, and well-formed recovery output. Use the already passing Asashio fixture for a lifecycle-isolation recovery case, while preserving a separate reported-screenshot exact-accuracy case. The end-to-end acceptance stage must remain failed until its exact-field requirement passes.
3. Diagnose the targeted field problems identified above without hardcoding ground truth or weakening validation: reported-image crop geometry / PSM 11 candidate formation; Hoffmann map/reward parsing; Libertad name/reward/damage boundaries; Schlieffen map/XP/Free XP. Keep uncertain critical fields missing for manual correction rather than guessing.

6. Rerun the complete Windows Node 24 acceptance suite on corrected current source. Require all 10 stages to pass and exact expected fields on all fixtures. [Current runtime authorization: Node.js v22.16.0 is acceptable; Node.js 24+ is not a prerequisite. Preserve this historical result as recorded.]

**Decision:** RC2 Gate A passes; current OCR acceptance Gate B fails. The injected-stall termination and fresh-child cleanup sequence are supported by the exact report, but the combined recovery acceptance fails on damage ambiguity. **Decision: Gate A PASS; Gate B FAIL/OPEN; do not proceed to first/middle/final Phase 3 fault-injection expansion or production release yet.** The fresh user-run Windows log supersedes the older statement that RC2 target-runtime validation had not yet been run. This does not establish OCR acceptance or recovery correctness.

### User-provided Windows RC2 results (`Windows PowerShell5.txt`)

- Runtime: Node.js `v24.22.1`, npm `11.19.1` (meets the package requirement `>=24.17.0`).
- `npm ci`: passed; 39 packages added, 40 audited, zero vulnerabilities reported.
- `npm ls --depth=0`: passed with `discord.js@14.27.0`, `dotenv@16.6.1`, `tesseract.js-core@6.1.2`, and `tesseract.js@6.0.1`.
- `npm test`: **104 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped** (106 total). The skipped cases explicitly describe POSIX-only process-group fixtures; the Windows cancellation cases appear in the passing watchdog suite.
- `npm run test:ocr-watchdog`: **14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**.
- `npm run test:tesseractjs`: **3/10 acceptance stages passed**; no blocked network attempts were reported. The two skipped unit-test cases above are distinct from this acceptance result.

### Interpret the 10-stage acceptance result carefully

1. **Unit/contract tests:** pass (35 tests).
2. **Local-asset package check:** pass; all listed local assets present, 3,954,569-byte asset hash `9d7c43fb206dc9f48475228b46bf35f888fa9e6259da2e67d5a75c77049f2dc7`.
3. **Deliberately wrong expected-value negative control:** wrapper later reports `PASS ... verified=true` after the injected comparison correctly yields `exact=false`. Treat the negative control as passed according to that explicit verification, despite the child-level `FAIL` line that reflects the deliberately wrong value.
4. **Integrated real-recognition stall/recovery:** failed. The log reaches the recovery child, reports confirmed worker termination with exit code 1, and the child closes after about 25 seconds; the harness then prints `false !== true`. The excerpt does not include the full generated JSON/error details, so the precise failing assertion/cause must be confirmed from `integrated-stall-recovery-.../report.json` and the full stage log. Do not claim recovery passed.
5. **Reported screenshot/no-network accuracy:** failed. The excerpt preserves a damage ambiguity of `873131` vs `87373`; expected damage in the harness is `87373`. The system declined to resolve the conflict rather than silently selecting a value. This is an accuracy/candidate-agreement failure, not the old instant preload launch error.
6–8. **Three five-image sequential runs:** each completed all five image jobs but only **1/5 was exact and 1/5 had complete required fields** in each run. The visible tail repeatedly shows `libertad-victory.jpg` missing two fields and `schlieffen-defeat.jpg` missing one. Repetition across all three runs points to repeatable fixture-specific errors, not a one-off order/flakiness result. Read each JSON report to identify all failing images and field-level differences.
9–10. **Hoffmann/Asashio in both orders:** Asashio passed exact with all required fields in both orders; Hoffmann failed with one missing required field in both orders. The same Hoffmann failure regardless of position suggests a fixture-specific extraction/validation problem rather than evidence of queue order contamination. Full per-image JSON is needed before choosing a code fix.

The visible log reports `blocked network attempts=no`; record this as no observed blocked attempts in this run, not as proof that the seven failing acceptance stages passed. The harness progressed beyond its earlier immediate preload error, but the required OCR acceptance gate is not close to passing yet.

### Gate status and next action

- **Gate A — target-runtime install and regression baseline: PASS for RC2.** Preserve this exact Windows log. This supersedes the earlier Linux-only preflight limitation for install/full-suite/watchdog. The log is sufficient to report these commands passed on the target runtime.
- **Gate B — OCR acceptance: FAIL / OPEN.** Do not change OCR logic based only on this summarized excerpt. Compare actual/expected values and missing-field reasons, preserving source/runtime/model metadata. Confirm whether the recovery child's payload has `OCR_OPERATION_TIMEOUT` or a different failure code; determine why the clean recovery child terminated its worker before asserting recovery success. Distinguish timeout/reliability failure from field accuracy mismatch.
- **Phase 3 continuation: still paused for feature expansion.** Gate A passed, but Gate B's real stall/recovery test failed and screenshot acceptance remains substantially inaccurate. Diagnose these existing failures first. Once baseline failure details are understood and the acceptance path is reliable, run controlled stall-at-first/middle/final recognition cases against the corrected current source. Analyze historical pass-16 logs independently; leave root cause unknown unless those logs prove it.

In parallel, correct the identified reported/Hoffmann/Libertad/Schlieffen field-specific problems in a separate work copy; then rerun all 10 stages and require exact matches. Investigate historical pass-16 separately and do not infer its root cause from pass-2 injection. **Production remains NO-GO.**

### Required claim audit — frozen Phase 0 findings (historical snapshot)

The following findings describe the **Phase 0 candidate as it existed before Phase 1 changes**. They are retained to preserve the audit trail; they are not descriptions of the current Phase 1 working source. Current outcomes are recorded in the reconciled status at the top of this plan and in `PHASE-1-IMPLEMENTATION-REPORT.md`. Do not delete or rewrite this historical evidence; compare it with the new reports instead.

1. **Reported-screen damage consensus:** The current damage-selection path first builds candidates from focused-crop readings. Full-screen PSM 3/PSM 11 candidates are fallback-only when focused-crop candidates are empty; it does not implement the claimed rule that agreeing independent full-screen reads take precedence over conflicting crop consensus. The fresh reported-screen baseline returned damage `87313`, expected `87373`, with 8/9 expected fields matched. **Claim not present as described; accuracy acceptance fails.**
2. **Independent per-operation watchdog:** `runOcr` calls `worker.setParameters()` and `worker.recognize()` directly. The application checks an overall image budget between operations and the serial queue has a task/image timeout, but no independently enforced watchdog races each `setParameters`/`recognize` operation. **Not present.**
3. **Worker recycling after every successful image:** `getOcrWorker()` returns a module-level cached worker when available. The successful-image path does not terminate it; termination occurs on timeout/shutdown. **Not present.**
4. **Fail-closed cleanup:** The serial queue contains fail-closed handling when a task or cleanup callback fails to settle within the grace period. However, the pinned Tesseract.js 6.0.1 Node termination wrapper invokes the underlying worker's terminate call without returning/awaiting its termination promise; awaiting the public wrapper does not prove worker-thread exit. **Partial; actual worker exit is not proven.**
5. **Local language/worker/core asset resolution:** Explicit local asset paths and unit tests for resolving local paths and reporting a missing language model exist. **Present in code; actual no-network end-to-end initialization and recognition has not been run.**
6. **Reported screenshot 9/9 claim:** The latest preserved fresh fallback run matched 8/9, with the damage mismatch above. **Not verified; current recorded run fails.**
7. **Four-image fallback baseline:** The fresh sequence completed 4/4 pipeline jobs, but exact expected-field match was 0/4 and required fields were complete in only 1/4. This is evidence that pipeline completion/nonempty values do not establish OCR correctness. **Accuracy acceptance fails.**
8. **Native OCR comparison:** Direct Linux `/usr/bin/tesseract` version 5.5.0 PSM 3 read the reported screenshot damage as `87373`. The full bot native reported-screenshot pipeline did not complete within the recorded 120-second tool window and produced no report. This is **inconclusive**, not a native-pipeline pass and not Windows validation.

## Phase 1 — Fix the failing real-dependency test and establish a clean test baseline

**Status: IMPLEMENTATION COMPLETE; TARGET WINDOWS ACCEPTANCE PENDING.** The panel test now inspects the serialized payload from real discord.js 14.27.0 builders via `.toJSON()`, preserving assertions for custom IDs, values, labels, totals, event-selection behavior and channel isolation. Malformed/missing/unsupported interaction input is covered. Two cross-platform cancellation tests now use real hanging Node child processes through the production cancellation path and assert closure, rather than treating a kill request as proof of exit. Latest `npm test` in the available Linux environment: **102 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, Node.js v22.16.0. The supplied Windows transcript is historical evidence for the earlier candidate, not a run of this updated source. Run the exact PowerShell acceptance commands in `PHASE-1-QUICKSTART-POWERSHELL.md` before closing target Windows validation.

### 1.1 Diagnose the interactive panel failure

Investigate `tests/full-system.test.js:817` and the implementation of the public interactive panel.

Determine which object is undefined and why the test expects it to contain an array. Check the actual discord.js 14.27.0 builder APIs and serialized component structures.

Then:

- Correct the test or implementation according to the intended behavior.
- Use actual discord.js builders and serialization rather than shims.
- Avoid depending on undocumented internal object shapes.
- Retain assertions for totals, event selection, correct responses and rejection of interactions originating from other channels.
- Add checks for malformed, missing or unsupported interaction data.
- Verify that the panel still works with the existing prefix-command interface.

Do not weaken the test or remove its assertions merely to produce a passing suite.

### 1.2 Address the two skipped Windows process tests

The skipped tests use POSIX-specific process-group behavior. Determine whether equivalent Windows child-process tests already exist.

Where appropriate, add Windows-specific tests that verify actual process cancellation and termination. The tests must check the child's `close`/exit result rather than treating the act of sending a kill request as proof that termination occurred.

Retain the POSIX fixtures for POSIX CI environments. Clearly distinguish platform-specific skips from the Windows tests that replace their coverage.

### 1.3 Phase 1 acceptance criteria

Run:

```powershell
node --version
npm ls --depth=0
npm test
```

Require zero failing tests. On Windows, POSIX-only fixtures may be skipped only when separate Windows-specific tests prove equivalent behavior by observing child `close`/exit and confirming cancellation/cleanup; a comment that Windows uses a native kill path is not enough evidence by itself. Keep the POSIX tests for POSIX CI, and keep both platform results separately identifiable. Add tests for malformed, missing and unsupported interaction data if not already covered.

The panel correction is already verified in the candidate-specific Windows transcript and the available Linux run; do not redo it unless later code changes invalidate that evidence. Save new full test output, exit code, runtime/dependency versions and report hashes after any changes. Do not continue to interpret a passing suite with unverified process termination as proof of the whole fallback's reliability.

## Phase 2 — Build a repeatable Tesseract.js fallback acceptance command

**Status: IMPLEMENTED; LOCAL ACCEPTANCE PASSED; WINDOWS NODE 24 VALIDATION PENDING.** The cross-platform Node launcher is `scripts/accept-tesseractjs.js`, exposed as `npm run test:tesseractjs`. ## Phase 3 — Prove and correct sequential OCR, timeout, cancellation and cleanup

**Status: IMPLEMENTED; LOCAL ACCEPTANCE PARTLY PASSED; HISTORICAL ROOT CAUSE AND TARGET-WINDOWS VALIDATION OPEN.** The current source adds operation watchdogs for Tesseract.js `setParameters` and `recognize`, waits for the actual underlying worker thread termination result on timeout, and runs each image in a separately supervised child process. The parent imposes an independent image deadline, kills the child tree on timeout, and waits for the `close` event before advancing. Tests for a stuck operation, worker-termination confirmation, fail-closed behavior, real hanging child cancellation, and queue recovery passed in the contract suite. Three five-image network-blocked runs passed 5/5 each; the two image orders passed 2/2 each; and the separate four-image Tesseract.js run passed 4/4. However, the historic pass-16 stall is not proven to have a single identified root cause. An integrated injected stall through an actual Tesseract.js recognition pass followed by a successful real-image recovery, plus the target Windows Node.js 24 run, remains necessary before this phase is fully closed. [Current runtime authorization: Node.js v22.16.0 is acceptable; Node.js 24+ is not a prerequisite. Preserve this historical result as recorded.]

This phase must establish a reliable explanation for the historical stall on the second image at recognition pass 16. Do not assume that it is caused by worker termination unless the evidence supports that conclusion.

### 3.1 Instrument the complete lifecycle

Capture run ID, image job ID, queue position, worker initialization, each OCR pass, crop type, crop dimensions, PSM, preprocessing settings, recognition start/end, timeout firing, termination request, confirmed termination, promise settlement, parsing, validation and report writing.

Capture the last completed operation before a stall. Avoid exposing credentials or unnecessary user data in routine logs.

### 3.2 Reproduce the sequential problem

Run the Hoffmann and Asashio fixtures in both orders, then the four-image batch, then the five-image batch.

Repeat each required sequential scenario at least three times. Compare with separate-process executions of the same fixtures.

Inject a controlled stall at the first, middle and last recognition pass. Verify that the timeout fires and that the failed task cannot overlap a subsequent task.

Identify whether any stall originates in recognition, parameter updates, event-loop behavior, preprocessing, parsing, report writing, cleanup or the outer test runner.

### 3.3 Implement robust cancellation

Review the actual Tesseract.js 6.0.1 worker and termination code.

If its termination API cannot establish that the underlying worker has exited, implement the smallest safe alternative. A separately supervised per-image OCR child process is acceptable if needed, provided the parent can enforce an independent deadline and wait for the child to close.

The job queue must:

- Have a bounded backlog.
- Report queue position and explicit overflow.
- Use per-operation and per-image deadlines.
- Kill and wait for the stalled worker/process.
- Confirm closure before starting another image.
- Settle pending promises and release resources.
- Halt and report a fatal error if cancellation cannot be confirmed.

Do not simply race recognition against a timer and assume the underlying computation stopped.

### 3.4 Phase 3 acceptance criteria

- A forced 30-second timeout produces an explicit application-level timeout within a documented deadline plus cleanup grace period.
- Worker/process termination is confirmed.
- A subsequent image succeeds after an ordinary timeout with successful cleanup.
- No subsequent image starts while the prior worker is still active.
- Both image orders and three consecutive sequential batches pass.
- A cleanup failure stops the queue safely.
- No timeout silently creates an incomplete correction workflow or a false successful record.

Begin with a 90-second per-image diagnostic budget and a provisional three-minute batch target, then measure performance on the target Windows machine before making production settings permanent.

## Phase 4 — Correct Tesseract.js field accuracy and prevent false acceptance

**Status: LOCAL TESSERACT.JS ACCEPTANCE PASSED; TARGET WINDOWS VALIDATION PENDING.** Current exact-match evidence: reported screen 9/9; three five-image batches each 5/5; both Hoffmann/Asashio orders each 2/2; separate four-fixture Tesseract.js sequence 4/4. The reported damage now matches `87373`; numeric selection includes agreement/crop-geometry safeguards for damage, Credits, XP and Free XP, with regression tests. Historical incorrect outputs remain preserved in Phase 0 baseline reports and must not be overwritten. A target-runtime Windows run remains required before final release sign-off.

All five required screenshots must match their expected data exactly.

### 4.1 Preserve and diagnose the historical mismatches

The recorded baseline includes:

- Reported screenshot damage: `87313` instead of `87373`.
- Hoffmann XP: `14` instead of `14390`, incorrect Free XP and missing map, start time and damage.
- Asashio damage: `119717` instead of `171577`, with player/map missing.
- Libertad damage: `221678` instead of `227678`, with player and XP missing.
- Schlieffen Free XP: `3255` instead of `325`.

A later change reportedly fixed the reported screenshot by preferring two independent agreeing full-screen reads over conflicting focused-crop reads. Verify that code and its tests exist in the current source. Do not assume every other mismatch is fixed.

### 4.2 Separate recognition errors from parser errors

For each mismatch, compare the original image, crop, raw OCR output, recognition parameters, candidate values, selected field value and expected value.

Determine whether the problem is clipping, preprocessing, model recognition, numeric parsing, candidate selection or field mapping.

Keep diagnostic crop images and raw candidate outputs separate from the ordinary regression reports.

### 4.3 Compare engines fairly

If native Tesseract CLI is available, compare it and Tesseract.js on identical images and crops, with equivalent OEM/PSM and preprocessing where practical.

Record both engine versions and language-model sources. Keep the native and JavaScript report sets separate.

Native Tesseract should serve as a diagnostic control, not as a substitute for the fallback's own exact-match requirements.

### 4.4 Fix numeric and metadata errors

Use controlled experiments for:

- Damage digits and complete crop boundaries.
- XP and Free XP truncation or concatenation.
- Reward columns, separators and grouping.
- Player/clan name clipping.
- Map, mode and battle-start metadata.
- Crops, scaling, contrast, thresholding, borders, PSM and numeric whitelists.

Do not increase OCR pass counts without evidence of improved accuracy.

Separate these states:

- Detected: a candidate value exists.
- Consistent: independent evidence agrees.
- Verified: the candidate passes explicit acceptance rules.

A nonempty value must never be treated as correct merely because it exists. Uncertain critical values must enter the correction workflow.

### 4.5 Phase 4 acceptance criteria

- Reported screenshot: all 9/9 expected fields match.
- Each of the four fixtures: all expected fields match.
- Five-image batch: all five pass sequentially in one process.
- The five-image batch passes three consecutive times.
- Wrong or ambiguous critical values cannot pass merely because they are nonempty.
- Native OCR regressions and all existing bot tests remain passing.
- Corrections do not change workbook scoring, player mapping, event mapping, or existing tournament behavior.

## Phase 6 — Add safe OCR diagnostic collection and evidence-based improvement

**Status: OPEN; not implemented as a verified collection system.** Baseline JSONs and reports currently provide retained test evidence, but they are not a complete user-correction-linked diagnostic/feedback pipeline with configurable retention, quotas, deletion and verified-label provenance. Do not describe the existing baseline reports as that system.

Design and, where safely possible, implement an optional local diagnostics directory such as:

`data/ocr-diagnostics/`

The system should capture enough information to understand and reproduce errors:

- Original screenshot or an optional controlled-retention copy.
- Content hash for deduplication.
- Dimensions and source/build identity.
- Engine/model versions.
- Crop boundaries, preprocessing and PSM.
- Raw OCR candidate fields, selected values and verification status.
- Processing times and failures.
- User-confirmed corrections.
- Link between initial OCR values and confirmed values.
- Source and verification status of each label.

### Required privacy and storage safeguards

- Local processing must remain viable; free external services may be used when necessary and acceptable, subject to provider/destination approval and privacy/retention review before any screenshot or crop transmission.
- Configurable screenshot retention.
- Storage quota, rotation and explicit deletion.
- Duplicate prevention.
- No external transmission before the specific provider/destination is approved; any approved transmission is limited to the minimum necessary field crop and retains primary/remote evidence separately.
- No credentials/tokens in logs.
- Minimal routine logging of unnecessary player information.
- Separate diagnostic data from public-channel responses and tournament snapshots.

Only manually reviewed or user-confirmed corrections may become verified ground truth. Never automatically learn from an unverified OCR result.

Use the reviewed examples to improve crops, preprocessing, field validation and candidate selection first. Consider custom Tesseract training or another offline OCR engine only if measured results justify the extra complexity.

Use separate training, validation and holdout datasets. Prevent the same source image or near-duplicate from appearing in both tuning and holdout sets.

### Phase 6 acceptance criteria

- Diagnostics identify the image, crop/pass, raw OCR candidate and final selected result.
- User corrections can become labelled regression fixtures.
- Unreviewed OCR outputs never become verified labels.
- Quota, deletion, rotation and retention settings are tested.
- Changes improve holdout accuracy without regressing the five required screenshots.

## Final deliverables and response format

Start the final report with a status matrix for Phases 0–8 using exactly these meanings: COMPLETE (acceptance evidence is present), PARTIALLY COMPLETE (some requirements pass but required coverage remains), OPEN (not implemented or not run), FAILING (a tested criterion currently fails), and BLOCKED (external environment/dependency prevents a specific test). A phase can include more than one status if different criteria differ. Do not collapse historical success, current failure, and environment blockers into a single green status.

Provide a report with these sections:

1. **Baseline:** Actual current source, package versions and PowerShell environment.
2. **Phase-by-phase changes:** Files changed and reasons for each change.
3. **Tests:** Exact command, environment, result and exit code.
4. **OCR accuracy:** Expected versus actual fields for each of the five screenshots.
5. **Sequential reliability:** Repeated run results, timeout evidence, cleanup confirmation and queue recovery.
6. **Native versus JavaScript comparison:** When both engines are available.
7. **Feedback-data system:** What was implemented, how data is retained/deleted, and how corrections are verified.
8. **Remaining blockers:** Exact reason and next action for each.
9. **Release:** New ZIP and its hash if the release gates pass.
10. **PowerShell quick start:** Exact commands for my Windows machine.
11. **Go/no-go decision:** Whether the fallback is ready for production, with reasons.

Clearly distinguish actual test results, historical claims, test simulations, code changes and remaining external acceptance tests.

================================================================================
PHASE 10 BASELINE RECONCILIATION — 2026-11-04
================================================================================

The Image Text Reader baseline includes desktop file selection, drag/drop, clipboard image, preview, in-browser Tesseract OCR, progress, editable text, copy/download/reset and status handling. The example's automatic Discord webhook POST is prohibited because a hard-coded webhook is a bearer secret. A safe no-backend fallback can produce a downloadable image/JSON submission for the participant to attach manually in Discord, but this is not automatic browser-to-bot delivery.

The workbook calculator baseline includes four events (Damage, Kills, Race, Grand Yolo), seven games, dashboard, roster, standings, totals, multipliers/pools, localStorage, JSON backup, responsive layout and print. The provided file includes sample players/data and roster/reset/clear/scoring controls; these must not be exposed to participants unless the existing bot authorizes them. The Player derivative must be read-only for other participants and must not present workbook seed data as live scores.

A static file:// HTML page cannot securely retrieve private current bot data, authenticate the player, send a command with the bot's credentials, or enforce admin permissions. It can support local preview/OCR assistance, data editing, JSON export/import and manual Discord attachment. Live authoritative refresh, direct secure transfer and protected Admin operations require a trusted server-side bridge (preferably within the existing Node.js bot process) with verified identity, server-side authorization, request/origin/CSRF protections, limits and audit provenance. Do not deploy an internet-accessible bridge without an agreed host/network/auth configuration. Continue independent offline UI work; pause only that backend-dependent execution until the deployment/identity decision is available.

The exact implementation.zip and the exact filenames score_report_imgToDiscord.html, score_report_imgDiscord.html and yolo_tournament_scoreCalculator.html were not found in the inspected Library/archive material. The two full HTML examples pasted by the user are therefore the primary design inputs; testdata/yolo_tourney_workbook_reference.html, scoreReport_dark_clipboard (1).html, backupWkbkBrowser.html and the workbook are supporting references. Do not claim the missing archive/files were inspected.

Discord documentation reviewed:
- Gateway lifecycle, intents, READY, resume and rate limits: https://docs.discord.com/developers/events/gateway
- HTTP REST rate limits and 429 behavior: https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits
- Application commands and guild-scoped testing: https://docs.discord.com/developers/interactions/application-commands
- discord.js 14.27 Client, ClientReady and shard lifecycle events: https://discordjs.dev/docs/packages/discord.js/14.27.0/Client%3AClass
- discord.js 14.27 GuildChannel.permissionsFor signature: https://discordjs.dev/docs/packages/discord.js/14.27.0/GuildChannel%3AClass

Implementation committed only to dwbotVis:
1. Gateway lifecycle observability: shardReady, shardDisconnect, shardReconnecting, shardResume and shardError, plus client error/warning logging. discord.js retains responsibility for heartbeat, reconnect and session resume; no second Gateway loop.
2. REST rate-limit observability: client.rest rateLimited events record method, normalized route, scope, global flag and retry delay in milliseconds. No token, request body, authorization header or full URL is logged. No hard-coded REST limits or competing retry loop.
3. READY and target-channel startup preflight: logs bot tag/user ID after ClientReady, fetches the configured channel, verifies a sendable guild text channel and ViewChannel, SendMessages (or SendMessagesInThreads for a thread), AttachFiles. Uses targetChannel.guild.members.me (GuildMember) for discord.js permissionsFor; fails closed if the member cannot be resolved. If preflight fails, storage is closed, client destroyed and process exit status set non-zero.
4. Admin command authorization correction: the plural read-only !yolo settings command was missing from the previous admin-only allowlist although its handler used that name. Centralized policy now protects both settings and setting; full-system.test.js has participant-denial assertions for both.
5. Local setup hardening: added .gitignore and safe .env.discord-test.example; no actual token or IDs are included. The quickstart grants only View Channel, Send Messages and Attach Files; Read Message History is not required by the current workflow.
6. Offline local preflight: added src/discord-test-preflight.js and scripts/preflight-discord-test.js. It validates Node >=22.16, development/test environment, non-placeholder token presence without outputting its value, snowflake syntax, JSON test storage, state-path containment, distinct state paths and disabled slash/experimental flags. It makes no network calls and cannot validate token validity, Developer Portal intent toggles or live permissions.

SERVER-SIDE DESKTOP BOUNDARY
src/desktop-bridge.js is IPv4 loopback 127.0.0.1 only. It uses Discord OAuth identify scope only, state nonce, HttpOnly/SameSite session cookies, CSRF token, same-origin and Host checks, request/upload bounds, image magic-byte validation, safe filenames, rate limits, CSP/security headers and audit logging. The Admin page and /api/admin/* operations are protected server-side.
src/desktop-service.js uses existing in-memory tournament state, save path, Tournament scoring, BattleRecord correction validation and the same extractText(imageBuffer) queue used for Discord attachments.
Players see only owned screenshot records and may map only an exact linked roster identity. Mismatches remain admin-review.
The bridge starts after Discord READY and configured-channel preflight only when ENABLE_DESKTOP_UI=1. Shutdown closes the bridge. Desktop Admin requires explicit TOURNAMENT_ADMIN_IDS; Manage Server alone is insufficient.

SHARED IMAGE / OCR WORKFLOW
Desktop image bytes enter extractText(imageBuffer), the same bounded serial/supervised Tesseract-first pipeline as Discord attachments. No separate OCR route is created and OCR.space remains disabled.
Discord-originated records from roster-linked submitters are stored in shared state with ownership and OCR data. Desktop corrections update the deterministic record identity and clear stale matching Discord prompts. Desktop mapping clears matching pending Discord mapping/correction associations.

PRIVATE VERSUS PUBLIC BACKUP CONTRACT
The complete private local state contains player_account_links, per-record desktop owner IDs and pending per-user workflows. It is returned only through authenticated local Admin GET /api/admin/backup.
Public Discord snapshots and public !yolo export / !yolo finalize use Tournament.makePublicArchiveSnapshot. They retain workbook-compatible scores and battle records but omit account links, per-record desktop owner identifiers, pending_submissions and pending_ocr_corrections. Public snapshots include omitted workflow counts and an explanatory note.
A public snapshot is not a complete private backup. Use authenticated local Admin backup for complete private recovery. Importing a public snapshot requires account relinking and restarting omitted pending workflows.

OCR / SCORING INVARIANTS
Nine mandatory fields and order remain: result, player, map, mode, battleStart, credits, freeXp, damage, xp. XP remains mandatory and ninth. No fuzzy player/name/value repair or weakened exact validation.
Tesseract.js remains baseline; OpenCV geometry only; PaddleOCR and OCR.space are secondary evidence. OCR.space remains disabled by default. No live OCR.space request or image transmission occurred.
Grand Yolo tierWeight remains 0.9. Workbook Totals!H12 remains =0.54*G12/100; known default is $12.49.

DWBOT-UPD-2026-11-06-004 — 64-IMAGE ALL-MEMBER PREFLIGHT BEFORE PROVIDER USE
================================================================================

The 64-image runner now performs a complete source-member preflight after
verifying the exact imgsHalf1.zip archive fingerprint and before its first
OCR.Space request. It reads and SHA-256/size-verifies all 64 extracted member
files into a bounded in-memory buffer set, then starts the per-image processing
loop. This prevents a late member mismatch or missing file from being
discovered only after earlier images have already been transmitted.

The preflight covers all 64 records, including the eight user-excluded images.
After successful preflight, the processing loop skips those eight records with
providerSubmission=false and only sends the 56 eligible images to Engine 2.
The report records sourceMemberHashesVerified=64 and
allSourceMembersVerifiedBeforeProvider=true. A regression test asserts the
all-member preflight precedes the first provider call.

No archive was present and no OCR.Space request was made. The code change is
subject to the exact-head Actions run and must not be treated as verified until
the new unit test passes. The OCRSPACE_API_KEY Actions secret remains missing;
the source archive and extracted image root also remain required. Accuracy
remains not evaluable without independent nine-field ground truth.

END DWBOT-UPD-2026-11-06-004

================================================================================
DWBOT-UPD-2026-11-06-005 — 64-IMAGE MEMBER PATH/SYMLINK PREFLIGHT
================================================================================

The all-member preflight now resolves every extracted member with fs.realpath
and verifies the resolved target remains inside the canonical source root before
reading or hashing it. A symlink or path that resolves outside the approved
image root fails closed before the first OCR.Space request. Added a regression
contract test for the realpath containment guard. No image was uploaded.

- Network diagnostics hardening: the current workflow now initializes and
  uploads imagesF-63-blocked-network-attempts.log via OCR_NON_OCR_NETWORK_LOG.
  This change cannot affect the already-running pinned workflow. Its network
  evidence must be reconstructed from job logs and localReaderFailures; if
  zero unauthorized attempts cannot be established, Gate B stays blocked.
- Bounded-read review also identified a post-allocation size check in the
  non-streaming OCR JSON response.json fallback, in addition to the PDF
  arrayBuffer fallback. Track both as hardening opportunities; streaming
  runtime paths are bounded.

- Intake/worker source audit completed: Discord download is streamed/cancelled
  at 25 MiB with a 30-second timeout; desktop uploads are 25 MiB with a
  120-second request timeout, 15-second header timeout and max-two concurrency
  (default one); the serial queue is single-consumer and fails closed if worker
  cleanup/settlement cannot be confirmed; the process supervisor kills child
  trees and waits for close confirmation. Remaining hardening opportunity:
  desktop chunked uploads are drained after crossing the byte cap until request
  completion/timeout rather than being immediately terminated. No source code
  was changed for this audit.

- Gate A evidence limitation: the evaluator validates the Gate A attestation
  fields and SHA-256 formats but does not parse the original Gate A report.
  Retain and manually inspect that report, verify its hash, and compare its
  locked candidate fingerprint with the Gate B run source. Without the actual
  source report, Gate A evidence remains pending; do not self-attest PASS.

- Scoring parity correction: standalone HTML calculator Grand Yolo tierWeight
  now defaults and resets to 0.9, matching src/tournament.js and admin UI;
  legacy 0.9 values are preserved instead of silently converted to 1.
  Regression assertion added in tests/tournament.test.js. Exact-head CI and
  browser validation remain pending.
